# STATUS — FR3 handover bake-off

Live handoff log. **Append one dated line after every completed step**, then
`git commit && git push`. The orchestrator on the Mac reads only this file and
the tmux pane. Gate verdicts go in as `GATE PN: PASS|FAIL`; a stop condition
goes in as `BLOCKED: <reason>`.

Format: `- YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM  <what happened>  [run=<path>] [rc=<n>]`

Error grows along the clip (0.39 / 0.60 / 0.89 / 0.92 / 0.83 rad per fifth); holding the first frame would score 0.77. Cause: SONIC's rewards are body-position/orientation kernels tuned for a humanoid (std 0.3 m / 0.4 rad) and there is no joint-space term, so a 7-DoF arm is free in its null space; the tracked link origins sit on the joint-1/7 axes. Consequence for P3: the encoder's tokens carry hand poses, so B-oracle joint replays will look like this unless a joint-space reward is trained in (variant jp2 running) — a decoder that cannot replay the demos' joints is itself lane B's early answer.

- 2026-09-03 22:06  P5 SECOND CROSS-ENV GAP = THE START TRANSIENT. Traced B-oracle of jp20 ckpt 1000 on table episodes 4-8 in the handover env (by hand with the allocator, ORACLE_B_TRACE_DIR=/tmp/franka-sonic/lane_b/p5_trace_jp20_1000 (instance-local), same protocol, 21:57-22:00 UTC, results 2/5: eps 4,5 success, 6,7 at 0.667, 8 at 0.167; analysed with harness/lane_b/p5_trace_fk.py + /tmp/franka-sonic/lane_b/p5_trace_approach.py): the env does start at the demo's frame-0 joints (gap <= 0.07 rad; frame 0 itself is ~0.65 rad from FR3_READY), but in the first 25 frames of EVERY episode the decoder's targets are 9-11 cm / 0.2-0.7 rad off the demo (and the plant cannot follow them, |meas(t+1)-target(t)| 8-11 cm); the hand settles to 1-2 cm only after ~1.5 s. Demos whose left grip closes early miss (demo_8: close at frame 98, hand 6.7 cm off, approach window 7.7 cm mean / 15 cm max; demo_4: close at 73), demos closing at frame 258 (6, 7) are at 1.2-1.7 cm and then fail only at the right grasp. Cause: the SONIC env resets ON the reference WITH the reference joint velocity (commands.py: joint_vel = motion-lib vel + U(joint_velocity_range) and our joint_velocity_range is [0, 0]; joint_position_range [-0.05, 0.05]); the handover env starts the arm at rest, a state the policy never saw (the demos move at ~1.2 rad/s from frame 0). Per-clip SONIC-env replays of the failing episodes' own clips (d4/d8/d11, jp20 ckpt 1000: 0.044-0.057 rad, body 0.006-0.009 m [decoder_replay-44/-45/-46]) are clean, as expected for an on-reference start. Fix on the training side (legit for the P6 server too, whose first tokens arrive with the arm at rest): jp22 = jp20 + `manager_env.commands.motion.joint_velocity_range: [-1.5, 1.5]` and `joint_position_range: [-0.1, 0.1]` (harness/lane_b/sonic_dual_fr3_jp22.yaml) RUNNING: ~/runs/franka-sonic/lane_b/2026-09-03_sonic_rl-16 launcher pid 724609 trainer wrapper 725197 device 4, --num-envs 4096 --hours 1.0 from jp20 model_step_002000.pt, launched 22:04, cap ~23:04 UTC. jp20 itself runs to 22:33 (ckpt 2000 export/replay in progress).

    WP 7.1 FINDING - THE EVALUATION SPAWN RANGE IS NOT +/-6 cm / +/-0.5 rad. Measured from the code: the evaluation env `Isaac-Stack-Cube-DualFranka-JointPos-v0` (DualFrankaJointPosEnvCfg -> DualFrankaCubeStackEnvCfg) draws the cube from `_block_spawn_event` = **+/-0.015 m in x and y, +/-0.4 rad yaw**, centred on START_POS = (ROBOT_POS[0], SLOT_Y) = (0.4375, -0.78), with `env.reset(seed=eval_args.seed + episode)` (evaluation/eval.py:1520) and no eval-side override of events.init_cube (eval.py touches only the donut task's events). Arm start: events.reset_chain/reset_chain_2 noise_std = RESET_NOISE_STD_RAD = 0.02 rad on BOTH arms (only the donut task zeroes it). The +/-6 cm / +/-0.5 rad in PLAN.md is the GENERATION range (mimic/env_cfg.py GEN_SPAWN_XY=0.06 / GEN_SPAWN_YAW=0.5), i.e. round 1's training set ALREADY covered the evaluation range with ~4x margin in x/y and 1.25x in yaw - verified by measuring round 1's export (shared/2026-09-03_demos-2, 80 episodes): x half-range 0.0584, y 0.0565, yaw 0.4883, means (0.4423, -0.7811, -0.040), covers_eval=true.
    WP 7.1 DECISIONS: (a) widen GENERATION to +/-0.09 m / +/-0.75 rad (the prompt's target) = 6x / 1.9x the evaluation range, via MIMIC_SPAWN_XY / MIMIC_SPAWN_YAW read by harness/data/generate_handover.py's existing Mimic-env-cfg patch - the evaluation env never instantiates that class, so the eval binding/seeds/rate/replan/horizon are untouched and round-2 numbers stay comparable with round 1's. Trade-off recorded: at +/-9 cm only ~1/36 of the episodes land inside the evaluation box (vs ~1/16 at round 1's +/-6 cm), but 1000 episodes make that ~28 in-box episodes vs round 1's ~5. (b) also widen the initial ARM pose: MIMIC_ARM_NOISE_STD=0.05 rad (2.5x the evaluation's 0.02) on both arms' reset_chain events, generation only. (c) the handover PLACEMENT point is deliberately NOT randomised: SLOT_CENTER_X is baked into the `left_placed` subtask term and into handover_success/the rubric, which the EVALUATION also reads - moving it would change the evaluation, which this phase may not do (prompt WP 7.1). (d) out/coverage.json is produced by the new harness/data/spawn_coverage.py from the export shards' per-demo initial_cube_pose, with the evaluation range parsed out of the franka repo's own sources (not hard-coded); it is a new `coverage` sub-step of stage_demos, run before the replay check.
    WP 7.4 prep: the replay check now replays --n-replay RANDOM episodes (seeded draw over the replay_success pool that coverage.json measured, recorded in out/replay_episodes.json) via a new `--episode-indices` flag on harness/lane_a/eval_oracle_a.py; ALL of them must report handover_success=True or the stage fails.
    Other harness changes needed for the scale-up: generate_parallel.py and the export shards now pin one worker per claimed device round-robin over CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES (with a single device - round 1 - every worker gets that device, unchanged), and generate_parallel.py has `--deadline_min`: at the deadline it stops ITS OWN worker pids (SIGTERM, SIGKILL after 120 s) and merges what they flushed (isaaclab's RecorderManager.export_episodes flushes after every recorded episode, so a stopped worker's HDF5 is complete up to its last success); an unreadable worker file is skipped with a warning instead of aborting the merge.

    jp24 = jp20 with the round-2 motion library and NOTHING else (the P5 plant is not re-derived: dual_fr3_lanea = gravity off, PD 400/80 on all seven joints, armature 0, USD velocity limits, soft-limit 1.0, solver 16/1, 100 Hz physics decimation 2). jp26 = jp24 with ONE knob, the joint-space term's tight kernel 0.15 -> 0.08 rad; jp27 = jp24 with ONE knob, that term's weight 25.0 -> 40.0 (round 2 risks UNDER-fitting an 11.7x larger reference set, and round 1's failures were terminal-precision failures). Devices 2 and 3 are deliberately left free for the ceiling-test pipeline (export_onnx / label_tokens validate / oracle_b), one GPU-heavy job per device.

    READING. (1) **Both reward knobs hurt**: jp26 12/20 and jp27 13/20 against jp24's 17-18/20 at the same iteration. The P5 recipe is not improved by sharpening the joint kernel or by weighting it harder on the larger reference set — jp24 (jp20 + the round-2 library, nothing else) is the recipe, which is what the prompt's "do not improve the plant" warning predicted. (2) **Round 1's finding reproduces exactly**: replay error does not rank decoders — 0.045-0.049 rad spans 12/20 to 18/20, and the best replay (C1000, 0.045) is not the best ceiling. Only the 20-rollout test ranks. (3) **The ceiling is not monotone in iterations**: variant A went 18/20 at 500 to 14/20 at 1000 on a deterministic test set, so the selection has to sweep checkpoints, not just train longer. Current leader: B1000 (18/20, progress 0.958 — the P5 winner's round-1 progress on round 1's easier +/-6 cm episodes was also 0.958).

    Leaders after 11 tests: **B0500 and B1000 (both 18/20, progress 0.958)**, then A0500 and C1000 (18/20, 0.942). Four different checkpoints have now reached the prompt's >= 18/20 stopping value and every jp24 checkpoint tested at iteration <= 1000 is at or above the 15/20 gate floor.
    THE SERIES' OWN FINDING: **the B-oracle is not monotone in PPO iterations, and it is not predicted by replay error.** Variant A goes 18/20 (it 500) -> 14/20 (1000) -> 6/20 (2000) while its replay error sits at 0.047-0.049 rad throughout and its flange error never moves (1.1 cm); variant F goes the other way, 13/20 (1000) -> 17/20 (2000). Across all 13 tests the replay error spans 0.045-0.051 rad and correlates with nothing (the best replay, C1000's 0.045, is an 18/20; the worst, B0500's 0.051, is also an 18/20). This is round 1's finding reproduced on 13 checkpoints instead of 3, and it is the reason the phase selects on the 20-rollout test alone. Consequence recorded for P9/P10: a lane-B decoder must be chosen by a rollout test, never by a tracking metric, and "train longer" is not a lever here — the warm-started policy is at its ceiling within ~500-1000 iterations of the round-2 library.
    METHOD CAVEAT recorded honestly: picking the maximum of ~15-20 checkpoints on ONE 20-episode test set biases that maximum upward (a decoder whose true success is ~85 % will show 18-19/20 on some checkpoint by chance). The winner is therefore re-run on a WIDER 60-episode set before it is declared, and the protocol-identical 20-rollout run is done last so the gate reads the phase's stated bar.

- 2026-09-04 21:05  P8 — THE `label_tokens` MISMATCH, DIAGNOSED (orchestrator notes 18:10 / 18:45 / 20:15). The three questions, answered with measurements.
    **Q1 — does the ceiling pipeline consume tokens from a run whose check failed?** Yes, 12 of the 32 round-2 ceiling tests did; `encode` always completed and `check` was the only failing step. The affected ceiling numbers are listed below and are excluded from selection.
    **Q2 — is it benign? NO, but it is not the tokens.** In ALL 49 labelling runs of this campaign (17 round-1 + 32 round-2), `offline_vs_env.cmd_mf_max_abs_diff = 0.000`, `anchor_mf_max_abs_diff = 0.000` and `tokens.offline_vs_env_clean_frac_rows_identical = 1.000`. The failing clauses are only `decoder_onnx_vs_env_policy.median_row_max_diff` (0.156-0.264 vs 0.017-0.023 when it passes), `.rows_with_max_diff_gt_0.1` (719-818 of 818 vs 1-2) and `runtime.raw_action_vs_env_mean_abs_diff` (0.090-0.142 vs 0.041-0.056). Bimodal, nothing in between. Not `joint_pos_rel` (0.010-0.020, i.e. the env's own +/-0.01 observation noise) and not the command terms (exactly 0), so the 18:45 note's "then the tokens are wrong" branch is ruled out by measurement.
    **ROOT CAUSE — `harness/bakeoff.py lane_b/export_onnx` is NOT deterministic.** `export_onnx-4` and `export_onnx-28` name the SAME checkpoint (sonic_rl-4 `model_step_001000.pt`) in their `export_summary.json` and have different md5s. Diffing their ONNX initializers: **10 of 30 tensors differ, and they are exactly the g1 encoder's ten** (`module.encoders.g1.module.{0,2,4,6,8}.{weight,bias}`), by up to 0.024 against |w|max 0.15 — ~15 % relative, not float noise. Consequences measured on 300 frames of one clip: the two encoders agree on **0.0 %** of token rows (max |diff| 1.3125 = 21 FSQ bins) and their decoders differ by 0.13 mean / 0.95 max in raw action. Two exports of `model_step_001500.pt` (export_onnx-16 and -19) were, by contrast, byte-identical — so the fault is intermittent per export run, not per checkpoint.
    **Why the token clause cannot see it:** `offline_vs_env_clean` runs the SAME encoder ONNX on the offline observations and on the env's observations, so it validates the offline observation pipeline (which is correct) and is blind to which weights the export captured. `decoder_onnx_vs_env_policy` is the only clause that compares the export against the env policy's OWN actions — i.e. **the check is an export verification**, and it is doing its job.
    **Everything else is deterministic**, measured, not assumed: the SONIC validate env reproduces bit-for-bit (env_dump.npz of an OK run and a MISMATCH run of the same checkpoint: max|diff| = 0 on all 15 arrays, including the env policy's own `raw_action`); re-running `label_tokens.py check` twice on identical inputs gives identical numbers (medrow 0.2396, 818, 0.0994 both times); the B-oracle itself reproduces exactly when both runs used a verified export.
    **Q3 — why round 2 and not round 1?** It is not the reference set, not `--max-episodes` and not the screened-episode skip: 20 of the 32 round-2 runs pass with numbers indistinguishable from round 1's, `--max-episodes 20` runs appear in both groups, and the same checkpoint lands in both groups on different runs (D1000: label_tokens-5 OK, -32 MISMATCH). Round 1 ran 17 exports and drew a good one every time; round 2 ran 32 and drew 12 bad ones. With ~1/3 incidence, 17 clean draws in a row has probability ~0.001 — so round 1 was lucky, OR the incidence rose with today's parallelism (11 of the 12 mismatches came from tests that ran while other exports ran concurrently). Both readings say the same thing: **verify every export against the env policy before using it**, which `check` already does.
    **THE CHECK PREDICTS NON-REPRODUCIBILITY, 9 cases out of 9.** For the three checkpoints whose 20- and 60-episode runs BOTH had a verified export (D1500, Dlast, F2000), the 60-episode run's first 20 episodes are identical to the 20-episode run's, verdict for verdict. For all six checkpoints where either run mismatched (A0500, B0500, B1000, C1000, D1000, D0500), they are not. So the orchestrator's 20:15 rule is not just safe, it is the correct rule, and it is now enforced in code: `harness/lane_b/p8_ceiling.sh` refuses to report a ceiling number unless the labelling run printed `VERDICT: OK`.
    CEILING NUMBERS MEASURED ON AN UNVERIFIED EXPORT — kept in the record, marked unreliable, NOT used for selection: B0500 18/20, D0500 17/20, C0500 15/20, C1500 15/20, E0500 12/20, A1500 6/20, and the 60-episode runs D0500w60 48/60, C1000w60 40/60, B1000w60 34/60, B0500w60 34/60, D1000w60 34/60, A0500w60 13/60. (A0500's 18/20 and its 13/60 are the same checkpoint measured through a verified and an unverified export respectively — the clearest single illustration of what a bad export costs.)

Round 1: 1216 clips / 474 716 frames from 76 demos. - 2026-09-04 21:45  P9 WP 9.0 — the fine-tune is parameterised. `harness/bakeoff.py run … finetune` gained `--train-steps` (default 2000), `--save-steps` (default 500) and `--save-total-limit` (default 5); the round-1 numbers are the defaults, so every round-1 command still reproduces token-for-token. `--max-steps` is untouched and still means the EVALUATION horizon. `stage_finetune` now checks `checkpoint-<train-steps>` instead of the hard-coded `checkpoint-2000`.
    **3 GPUs per lane is impossible, and it is not NCCL.** `gr00t/experiment/experiment.py:55` asserts `global_batch_size % num_gpus == 0`; the rev-3c recipe fixes `--global-batch-size 32`, and 32 % 3 = 2. A 3-rank run dies on that assertion before a single collective is issued, so WP 9.1's NCCL smoke test cannot even be reached. The feasible divisors of 32 are 2 and 4 GPUs per lane.
    **DECISION: 4 GPUs per lane, both lanes concurrently, all 8 devices on the node.** Reasons: (i) with `--global-batch-size 32` fixed, 2 and 4 ranks are mathematically the same optimisation (global batch 32, no gradient accumulation) — only the per-device batch and the wall-clock differ, so nothing about the comparison changes; (ii) round 1 measured 1.29 s/it at 2 GPUs, i.e. 20 000 steps = 7.2 h per lane, which does not fit an 8 h session with 16 screening evals; (iii) the orchestrator's 17:55 note asked for exactly this — "P9 at 4 GPUs per lane and 30 000 steps with all 12 checkpoints per lane kept and screened afterwards".
    **COST OF THE DECISION, stated plainly:** with all 8 devices training there is no spare device to screen on, so WP 9.2's concurrent screening becomes screening-after-training and WP 9.3's early stop cannot fire — the trainers will run the full 20 000 steps. The stopping rule is still applied, as the selection rule it also is (best = highest success/20, ties to higher mean progress, further ties to the earlier checkpoint), and the report will say where the rule WOULD have stopped each lane. This is the honest trade: a complete 8-checkpoint series per lane inside the session, against an early stop that saves GPU-hours the phase does not otherwise need.
    Fallback if 4-rank throughput is no better than round 1's 2-rank 0.775 it/s: stop both trainers and relaunch at 2 GPUs per lane with 4 devices kept free for concurrent screening, which is WP 9.1's own named fallback. The first 200 steps are the smoke test — an NCCL fault shows there.

    **WHY 2 GPUS AND NOT 3 OR 4 — measured, not assumed.** (1) 3 ranks cannot run at all: `gr00t/experiment/experiment.py:55` asserts `global_batch_size % num_gpus == 0` and the rev-3c recipe fixes global batch 32. (2) 4 ranks faults: the fine-tune dies with `CUDA error: an illegal memory access was encountered` inside the NCCL watchdog ~40 s in, at the first collective after the `experiment_cfg` save, before the model is built — twice, in both lanes, in two independent attempts (run folders lane_a/2026-09-04_finetune, lane_b/2026-09-04_finetune, and both `…_finetune-2`). (3) **It is not the NCCL transport.** A 4-rank 256 MiB all_reduce on the same four devices (1,5,6,7) completes at ~100 ms/iter in all four configurations swept — sockets baseline (P2P+SHM off, i.e. exactly what `stage_finetune` sets), plus NVLS/CollNet off, plus `NCCL_ALGO=Ring NCCL_PROTO=Simple`, plus `NCCL_SOCKET_IFNAME=lo` — so the working transport round 1 found is fine at 4 ranks and the fault is in the trainer's own 4-rank path. The one difference between the probe and the trainer is that `_init_distributed_process_group` binds eagerly (`init_process_group(device_id=cuda:LOCAL_RANK)`); that is the first thing to test if a later round wants more than 2 ranks. Probe and sweep at /tmp/franka-sonic/p9/{nccl_probe.py,sweep_nccl.sh} (instance-local, not persistent); the finding is written into the comment above the NCCL env in `stage_finetune`.
    **THROUGHPUT AND THE CLOCK, stated plainly.** With both lanes training at once the step time is 1.67 s/it (lane A) and 1.42 s/it (lane B) against round 1's 1.29 s/it for a lane training alone — the two jobs share the node. That is an ETA of **9.2 h (A) and 7.8 h (B)** for the full 20 000 steps, against an 8 h session that started at 21:40. So: the trainers are `nohup setsid`-detached and survive this session; screening runs concurrently on the free devices from the first checkpoint on; and if WP 9.3's stopping rule fires (earliest possible: the 5th screened checkpoint, ~03:15 for B and ~04:10 for A) the phase finishes inside this session. That is not the screen. `watcher.py:newest_eval_run()` resolves a finished screening to the newest `*_eval*` folder that did not exist when it launched, and at 07:49/07:50 — while the screen was still running — I launched two 200-rollout P10 rows into that same lane's run root (eval-8 = ckpt-17500, eval-9 = ckpt-7500). The real screen is **eval-7** (`checkpoint-20000`, `rollouts: 20`, 20 complete CSV rows, its own log printing `success=16/20 progress mean=0.9333` at 07:59).     **Three things this phase measured that were not on the list, all in the report as computed sentences rather than prose:**
        (1) **The evaluation is not deterministic given the seed.** Lane A's `checkpoint-20000` scored 16/20 on its P9 screen and **11/20 on the identical first twenty seeds** inside its 200-rollout row; lane B's 17500 scored 13/20 and 12/20. GR00T N1.7 samples its actions, so a seed pins the spawn and not the rollout, and any two 20-rollout readings of one checkpoint differ by more than binomial noise on a fixed p. This is the strongest form of the "screens select, they do not measure" rule and it is now measured rather than argued.
        (2) **The screens overstated both headline checkpoints**, lane A by 43.5 points (16/20 = 80 % -> 36.5 %) and lane B by 19.0 (13/20 = 65 % -> 46.0 %).
        (3) **The two lanes fail in different places.** Lane A fails **70 of 200 episodes without reaching milestone 1 at all**; lane B fails 17 that way. Lane A's dominant failure is the arm's very first move — round 1's diagnosis (the policy copying the labels' saturated first-chunk differential-IK commands) survives 20 000 steps of training. Lane B's dominant failure is milestone 4 -> 5, the right arm's blind re-grasp (47 episodes), while its own B-oracle's is 5 -> 6, so lane B's dominant failure is **not** simply its decoder's.
